Fix the seed's declaration to match the measured truth; and check for other split pairs. D-20 F-3 — migrate.ts --target is a stop-after bound, not a select-one filter; D-9 part 2 corrected supersedes D-9 (part 2: the --target pinning CONDITION stands, but its stated reason was false — --target bounds the tail, not the head, and delivers the intent only in ascending one-migration-per-run order); D-18 (the dispatch's 589-before-588 sub-ordering is released)

2026-08-23T04:58:46Z · ADHIKARIN

OPTION A — ASCENDING ORDER, ONE MIGRATION PER RUN: --target 588_remove_asset_build_protection.sql, then --target 589_drop_orphaned_protection_functions.sql, then --target 590_nirmana_m0_catalogue_contract_columns.sql. THE 589-BEFORE-588 SUB-ORDERING IS RELEASED. The KARAKA was right to halt, right to refuse to reorder a stated safety mechanism on its own authority, and right about which option is best. (1) I CORRECT D-9 PART 2, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE IMPORTANT PART OF THIS RULING. I wrote that 590 must be applied with --target pinned 'so the run cannot sweep anything else.' THE CONDITION WAS RIGHT AND MY STATED REASON WAS FALSE. --target bounds only the TAIL of the run, never the HEAD (migrate.ts:625-681 iterates every collected file in global sorted order and applies each unapplied one, checking 'file.name === target' only AFTER the apply, to break; there is no filter on target anywhere in runMigrations, and collectMigrationFiles:400-411 merges both directories sorted globally by localeCompare). Since 590 is the HIGHEST-sorting unapplied file, '--target 590' excludes nothing whatsoever — it would have applied 588, 589 and 590. The KARAKA proved it live rather than by reading: a --dry-run pinned to 589 printed all three. So my ruling contained an ASSERTED PROPERTY WITH NO DETECTOR BEHIND IT — §N.8, arising inside a binding ruling rather than inside code. That is the fourth instance of this shape today and the first that is mine. I record it in exactly those terms because a ledger that catches the code's unearned claims while protecting its own is worth nothing, and because the fleet must stay willing to do to my rulings what this KARAKA just did. (2) WHY OPTION A IS CORRECT AND NOT MERELY AVAILABLE. --target DOES deliver D-9's intent, but only under the condition the KARAKA identified: files applied in ascending order, one run per file. Then each run's target is also the LOWEST unapplied file, so the loop applies exactly one migration and breaks — one migration, one ledger row, and the orders' after-check ('exactly one new _migrations_applied row') becomes meaningful instead of failing by construction. (3) THE COST OF RELEASING 589-FIRST IS EMPIRICALLY NEAR ZERO, WHICH IS WHY I RELEASE IT RATHER THAN RE-ENGINEER AROUND IT. The dispatch put 589 first so the flag would be rehearsed on a pure DROP ... The KARAKA independently re-measured count(*)=0 at 04:53Z. 588 is therefore just as safe a rehearsal as 589 today, PROVIDED the precondition still guards it. (4) THE PRECONDITION MOVES, AND THIS IS BINDING: D-10 part 4's check — build_protected_assets count(*) = 0 — is verified IMMEDIATELY BEFORE RUN 1 (588), not before a later step. The KARAKA's sharpest observation is that the dispatched sequence would have run 588's DELETE before the step where its own mandatory precondition sat: 'a precondition that is checked after the fact is not a precondition.' Exactly right, and that alone would have justified the halt even if --target had worked as I described. With Option A, 588 is first and its guard is checked immediately before it — the safety property is preserved, not traded away. (5) OPTIONS B AND C REFUSED. B (accept a two-migration first run) runs 588 before its precondition and destroys the one-row-per-run property that makes the after-check a detector at all. D-19 F-SEEDER-DEAD — which surface owns registry metadata; bg_cohort is a safety interlock supersedes D-17 (bg_cohort's disposition is unchanged — still untouched — but its rationale is strengthened: it is a safety interlock holding three hazards, not one)

2026-08-23T04:55:41Z · ADHIKARIN

ACCEPTED, AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS LARGER THAN EITHER REPORT STATED. I read the seeder's upsert rather than reason about it, and the decisive fact is in neither escalation: asset_registry_seed.ts:3215-3245's ON CONFLICT (asset_id) DO UPDATE SET INCLUDES target_floor, depends_on, count_sql, volume_explanation AND scope. Every one of those is a field §8.6 stage 2 Conform is REQUIRED to write, per rung, from measurement. SO THE HAZARD IS NOT DIVERGENCE, IT IS SILENT REVERSION: any seeder run after a rung's Conform stage overwrites that rung's measured floors, its DAG edges, its cockpit count_sql and its by-design volume_explanations with whatever the TS file happens to declare — no error, no warning, exit 0. That would destroy completed campaign work in a way nothing currently detects. (1) THE OWNERSHIP RULING, and I am reading it out of the plan rather than inventing it, because I7 has already decided it: a target_floor is set to the MEASURED ACHIEVED COUNT. A declarative source file structurally CANNOT hold a measured value — it can only hold a value someone typed, which is precisely the stale-historical-figure failure I7 exists to forbid. Therefore the line is: DECLARED values belong to the seeder (asset_id, layer, sort_order, names, descriptions, storage_type, target_table, asset_type); MEASURED and CAMPAIGN-ADJUDICATED values belong to the campaign and MUST NOT appear in the seeder's SET clause (target_floor, integrity_check_sql, domain, rung, partition declarations, consumers, and volume_explanation where it records a G4 by-design classification). Schema — the columns themselves — remains migrations'. This is not a new architecture; it is the only reading under which I7 and the seeder can both be true. (2) REQUIRED CHANGE, AND THE CODEBASE ALREADY CONTAINS ITS PATTERN. target_floor must be removed from the DO UPDATE SET clause or guarded so a re-seed cannot lower or overwrite a campaign-set value. THE PRECEDENT IS IN THE SAME SET CLAUSE, EIGHT LINES BELOW: the MR-06 RETIRED guard, which reads 'A RETIRED asset must NEVER be resurrected by a re-seed... preserve that status rather than blindly overwriting with whatever the seed says.' The shape is identical — a re-seed must never revert a measured floor — so the fix is to extend an existing, reviewed, in-file discipline rather than invent one. Same treatment for domain/rung/integrity_check_sql BEFORE they are ever added to the seeder; note they are currently safe only because 590 has not applied and the seeder does not yet know those columns exist. (3) THE SYNTHESIS THAT MATTERS MOST — bg_cohort IS A LOAD-BEARING SAFETY INTERLOCK, NOT A ONE-LINE BUG. Three separate silent-damage paths are TODAY held back by nothing but that formula error: (a) the import hazard M0-T15 closed, which would have reached INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE on asset_registry; (b) D-17's phantom DRY_RUN, where the documented command pairs the flag with $PROD_DB_URL and the flag does not exist; (c) this one, silent reversion of every Conform field. ALL THREE ARE UNLOCKED BY THE SAME ONE-LINE FIX. This is the third instance today of the shape I first recorded in D-9 — luck standing in for a guard — and it is now a pattern rather than a coincidence: this codebase repeatedly holds a hazard shut with something that was never meant to be a lock. RULING: bg_cohort's formula bug MUST NOT BE REPAIRED until all three hazards are independently closed. It stays pinned to rungs.R0.stage2_carry_forward as SUTRADHARA correctly placed it (I13 forbids touching it anyway, R0 being unopened), but its pin must now carry this warning, because the danger is that someone — an R0 KARAKA, or an unrelated session finding a broken seeder — repairs the obvious thing and silently arms three loaded mechanisms in one commit. (4) SCOPE: the seeder guard is machinery, data-neutral, and squarely M0 (catalogue reconciliation whose acceptance is contract conformance — a contract whose values can be silently reverted is not conformed). D-17 DRY_RUN=1 is a documented safety flag with no implementation; bg_cohort formula bug

2026-08-23T04:52:18Z · ADHIKARIN

VERIFIED AND WORSE THAN REPORTED, AND I AM TAKING NEITHER OPTION — there is a third that is better than both. WHAT I MEASURED MYSELF: 'DRY_RUN' occurs ZERO times in platform/scripts/seed/asset_registry_seed.ts. The archived brief does not merely mention the flag, it pairs it with production on the same command line — 99_ARCHIVE/BRIEFS_RETIRED/CLAUDECODE_BRIEF_L0_PHASE_ALPHA_v1_0.md:514 reads 'DATABASE_URL=$PROD_DB_URL DRY_RUN=1 npx tsx scripts/seed/asset_registry_seed.ts', and :637 instructs the operator to act on what the dry run reports. THE RULING — A THIRD OPTION: MAKE THE SEEDER REFUSE. If DRY_RUN is set in the environment and dry-run is not implemented, the seeder must EXIT NON-ZERO IMMEDIATELY with a diagnostic saying DRY_RUN is not implemented and refusing to run. A refusal at the point of danger protects the operator regardless of which document sent them, including ones neither of us has found. It is a handful of lines, it fails safe, it needs no new capability, and it converts a silent false promise into a loud honest one, which is this campaign's whole doctrine in miniature. D-15 D-13 part 3 condition discharged — git commit --only verified supersedes D-13 (part 3's verification condition discharged; --only is now binding rather than provisional)

2026-08-23T04:47:02Z · ADHIKARIN

CONDITION DISCHARGED — VERIFIED, and 'git commit --only' is now BINDING as the fleet's commit mechanism. I ran the actual test rather than reasoning about the man page, in an isolated scratch repo so no campaign state was risked. Setup reproduced the exact race: my file modified and unstaged, a sibling's file modified AND ALREADY STAGED in the shared index. Result of 'git commit --only mine.txt': the commit contained mine.txt ONLY — the sibling's staged file was NOT swept in; the sibling's staging SURVIVED INTACT (still staged after my commit); and the sibling's worktree content was unchanged ('SIBLING-INFLIGHT' preserved byte-for-byte). All three properties the fleet needs, confirmed by observation. The fallback I named in D-13 (the 'git diff --cached' check) is therefore not needed as a substitute, but it is RETAINED as belt-and-braces, because --only protects against a sibling's staged file while the diff check also catches the case where an agent has itself staged something it did not intend. Standing instruction, superseding the bare path-scoped rule in D-3 and the house rule in D-13: commit with 'git commit --only <explicit paths>'; never 'git add -A', 'git add .' or 'git commit -a'; and glance at 'git diff --cached' first. I record this as its own ledger line rather than quietly acting on it because D-13 made a promise about how the recommendation would be validated, and a condition that is discharged silently is indistinguishable from one that was forgotten.

D-14 The poke mechanism manufactures the stalls it detects — stall-state taxonomy corrected

2026-08-23T04:46:23Z · ADHIKARIN

YES, AND THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT FLEET FINDING SO FAR. THE DETECTOR WAS MANUFACTURING THE CONDITION IT DETECTED — a closed loop that would have produced 'stalls' indefinitely, escalating forever on evidence it created itself. That is not a monitoring bug; it is the campaign's own founding defect wearing fleet clothing, and it is the exact class §N.8 names: an action taken on a signal with nothing real behind it. It is also, as SUTRADHARA notes, the same defect as the launcher's original unsubmitted kick prompt — fixed in nirmana-up.sh, never fixed in the poke path. THREE BINDING RULINGS. (1) A NEW STATE EXISTS AND MUST BE DISTINGUISHED: IDLE-UNPOKED. A pane showing a COMPLETED turn with text sitting at the compose prompt is IDLE-UNPOKED, not stalled. Its remedy is to SUBMIT THE BOX — one keystroke. It is NEVER a restart. A restart here would have destroyed a live, healthy PARIKSAKA holding 13 queued reports and two filed verdicts, to fix a problem that needed an Enter. Byte-identical successive captures are evidence of this state, not of a hang: nothing was computing and nothing was going to. (2) A POKE MUST BE VERIFIED DELIVERED, and the mechanics are not the obvious ones. SUTRADHARA measured that a bare 'send-keys C-m' against a box already holding text DID NOT WORK — recorded here because the obvious fix fails and would otherwise be adopted and trusted. The sequence that works: send-keys C-u (clear any stranded fragment, which would otherwise be prepended to the next poke), sleep 1, send-keys '<text>', sleep 2, then send-keys Enter as a SEPARATE call after a settle. AFTER POKING, CAPTURE THE PANE AGAIN AND CONFIRM THE TEXT IS GONE FROM THE PROMPT. An unverified poke is an action with no detector behind it, which is precisely what this campaign exists to refuse — and PRAHARI must never escalate on silence that followed a poke it did not confirm landed. (3) PRAHARI'S CONDUCT WAS CORRECT AND IS COMMENDED ON THE RECORD. It escalated instead of restarting, which is what D-1(c) required, and that judgement is the only reason a healthy PARIKSAKA still exists with its context intact. Its escalation was right on the evidence available; the evidence was an artifact of the tooling, which is not a monitoring failure. This ruling does NOT relax D-1(c): PRAHARI still does not restart on silence. It now has a third option between 'ignore' and 'escalate' — submit the box and verify — and one more reason to distrust silence as a signal. D-1(c)'s suspension of restart-on-silence remains in force until the heartbeat defect is fixed AND a heartbeat has been observed from every agent.

FOUR PARTS. (1) I CORRECT MY OWN D-5. I ruled the collision 'SAFE... because the key is the filename, not the number', citing migrate.ts. That is true of the RUNNER and I verified it correctly, but I generalised it to 'safe' when I had only checked one of the two mechanisms that read these filenames. SUTRADHARA found the second: platform/scripts/ci/migration_number_guard.ts check E2 hard-fails on a duplicate LEADING NUMBER across both directories, precisely because the number 'is not a key of anything — it is a human coordination token, and nothing enforced it' (guard header, documenting the 467 precedent). So: SAFE FOR THE RUNNER, NOT SAFE FOR CI. The failure is deferred, not absent — it fires when both branches reach main, and on whichever PR merges second, which is not the PR that owns the bug. My D-5 'do not renumber' conclusion survives; my reason for calling it safe was too narrow, and a binding ruling that says 'safe' on a partial mechanism sweep is exactly the defect this campaign exists to remove. That is a verbatim description of this exact case. Using a guard's own documented, itemized disclosure route is using the guard as designed; it makes the collision VISIBLE and leaves the detector fully armed for every other number, which is the opposite of bypassing it. H3 forbids weakening a gate TO MAKE SOMETHING PASS; here the two alternatives are independently prohibited (H5, P2) and the mechanism's designers anticipated precisely this residue. D-8 M0 completeness — the §19 efficiency ledger (PARIKSAKA verdict V-1)

2026-08-23T04:16:01Z · ADHIKARIN

V-1 UPHELD. M0 IS NOT COMPLETE AND MUST NOT BE ADVANCED. NO DEFERRAL IS GRANTED — none is available, because this is M0's own spec, not a later campaign's. I checked the two clauses the answer turns on rather than accepting the verdict's characterisation, and both read verbatim as PARIKSAKA reports: §16 — 'NIRMANA_ASSET_CONTROL_WORKBOOK_v4_1.xlsx, REGENERATED IN M0 ... a new Efficiency sheet (per asset: p50/p90 baseline, rows/sec, bound class, hotspot, technique, target, achieved, identity proof — the §19 ledger)'. §15 — 'hotspot — the measured dominant cost, named; NULL UNTIL PROFILED', and 'One correction the regeneration must make: ... must emit a null hotspot rather than a reassuring sentence for any asset that has not actually been profiled — an unprofiled asset should read as unknown, not as fine.' THE ANTICIPATED OBJECTION FAILS. One could argue the efficiency VALUES cannot exist yet, since the profiling and output-identity harness §19 depends on is M3 content (§14.1, M3 row) — true, and irrelevant. §15 does not ask M0 for measured values; it asks for HONEST NULLS. The sheet with null hotspots IS the correct M0 deliverable, and M3 later fills what it measures. There is no sequencing conflict to defer around, so a G7 deferral here would defer work that is both required now and possible now — which is not a deferral, it is a green on an undone thing. WHAT MUST LAND IN M0: (1) the Efficiency sheet with §16's nine columns; (2) the five missing per-asset fields — bound class, hotspot, technique, identity proof, speedup achieved — as real emitted fields in the generators, not merely headings; (3) §15's correction: every asset not actually profiled reads NULL/unprofiled. ONE PRECISION WHERE V-1 OVERREACHES, and it binds the fix: not all 112 template sentences are false greens and they must not be swept away as one. 'No bespoke speed work' on an unprofiled asset IS a false green and is the H4/§N.8 violation V-1 correctly names — it asserts a finding about speed work that no profiler produced. But 'not-a-build; nothing to speed up' is a LEGITIMATE value: 'not-a-build' is one of §15's own enumerated bound classes, and for a service or reference asset it is true and derivable without profiling. 'Built once, reused by every chart' is likewise a true structural statement about a shared asset — though it is NOT a hotspot and must not occupy the hotspot field. So the repair is per-template and evidence-based: each sentence is kept only where a real detector or a structural fact produces it, and replaced with an honest null everywhere it stands in for 'we did not look'. Blanket-nulling all 112 would trade one unearned signal for another. (4) M0's note is corrected to PARIKSAKA's wording — scope/rung dimension done, §19 efficiency dimension not started. GATE CONSEQUENCE, recorded so it is not rediscovered at freeze time: §8.3 item 5 makes the efficiency pass a rung-freeze criterion and §16 names this sheet as the ledger it reads, so NO RUNG CAN FREEZE while the ledger has no emitter. R0 is blocked behind this today whether or not anyone has noticed. PARIKSAKA was right to raise it as a gate dependency rather than paperwork, and right not to fix it (I16).

D-7 I2 gochara snapshot — the restore-verification standard

2026-08-23T04:14:37Z · ADHIKARIN

PARTIALLY — and the gap is recorded now, before any operation depends on it. WHAT I VERIFIED MYSELF: the archive exists at 00_ARCHITECTURE/control/snapshots/20260823_pre_protection_removal/gochara_corpus_and_protection.dump at 16,214,137 bytes, byte-exact to 588's header claim; it is a valid PostgreSQL custom-format archive (v1.14-0, dumped from PG 15.18); its TOC is intact and carries the three entries that matter — TABLE DATA kala_gochara_windows, TABLE DATA build_protected_assets, and the id sequence. WHAT IS NOT VERIFIED, and must not be described as if it were: 588's header says the snapshot was 'verified restorable with pg_restore -l'. pg_restore -l READS THE TOC HEADER; IT DOES NOT READ, DECOMPRESS OR VALIDATE A SINGLE DATA BLOCK. A corrupt or truncated data section lists perfectly. 'Verified restorable' backed only by -l is a claim whose detector does not measure it — §N.8 exactly, and I hold the campaign's own snapshot to the standard the campaign imposes on its assets. SECOND, SCOPE CAVEAT: the archive is DATA-ONLY (TABLE DATA entries, no CREATE TABLE). It can restore rows into an existing table; it CANNOT recreate kala_gochara_windows from nothing. H1 forbids DROP/TRUNCATE on the corpus, so this is acceptable — but 'we have a snapshot' must never be read as insurance against losing the table itself. THE STANDING STANDARD, binding on every later G6 approval: BEFORE I approve ANY destructive operation touching kala_gochara_windows, a REAL RESTORE REHEARSAL MUST PASS — restore into a scratch database or schema, then compare row counts (expect 38,287 v1 and 1,884 gen-3.0, the figures I measured live today) and a content digest against production. Nothing less discharges G6's 'verified snapshot', and I will not accept pg_restore -l a second time now that I have said so. NOT BLOCKING TODAY: no rung is open, R3 owns gochara, and no destructive gochara operation is pending — so this is a precondition set in advance rather than a halt. Also recorded, since the migration is now the durable record: protection is GONE (I verified 0 triggers, 0 guard functions, 0 registry rows), so the corpus currently has no mechanical guard at all. P1 continues to reserve any operation on those rows to the native regardless of how good the snapshot proves to be.

(b) BINDING PRECEDENT on shared-tree git discipline, because five agents committing into one working tree is a real corruption hazard independent of this lock: (i) every agent stages with PATH-SCOPED 'git add <explicit paths>' ONLY — 'git add -A', 'git add .' and 'git commit -a' are FORBIDDEN fleet-wide, since they sweep another agent's half-written in-flight files into an unrelated commit; (ii) on index.lock, retry with backoff (6 x 5s) and only then inspect — a lock that is non-zero-byte OR younger than 10 minutes OR has a live git process behind it is LIVE: wait, never remove; removing a live lock corrupts the index; (iii) ledger commits for state/*.jsonl belong to LEKHAKA on a cadence, so the append-only ledgers have one committer and the other four agents commit only the artifacts of their own unit of work. This does not weaken _common.md's commit-per-unit-of-work rule; it scopes how the staging is done.
